Now README. Need insert after line 336. Use edit with context. Compose section. Need avoid emojis. Use heading `### Deploy the Auth Broker`. Content concise but sufficient.

Draft:

```
### Deploy the Auth Broker

The Broker is a FastAPI application in `auth_broker/`. Configure the GitHub App client and callback URL:

| Variable | Required | Description |
|:---|:---|:---|
| `GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_ID` | Yes | GitHub App client ID. |
| `GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_SECRET` | Yes | GitHub App client secret. |
| `BROKER_CALLBACK_URL` | Yes | Fixed HTTPS callback URL, for example `https://broker.example.com/auth/callback`. |
| `BROKER_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | No | GitHub token-exchange timeout in seconds. Default: `30`. |

Run the Broker with:

```bash
uvicorn auth_broker.app:create_app --factory --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000
```

Rate limits are enabled by default and stored in memory per process. Tune them for shared NAT environments:

| Variable | Default |
|:---|:---|
| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` | `true` |
...
```

Need maybe table with default and description. Too many columns? Fine.

`BROKER_TRUSTED_PROXIES`: "Comma-separated IPs/CIDRs of reverse proxies allowed to supply `X-Forwarded-For`. Leave empty when clients connect directly. When set, the Broker uses the rightmost untrusted forwarded address for per-IP limits." Good.

"Each process has independent counters; use shared storage or a single worker if strict global limits are required. Because limits are per IP, increase thresholds or use trusted proxy headers when many users share a NAT." Good.

"Blocked requests receive `429` with `Retry-After`; callback returns HTML." Good.

Need mention in-memory single process. Good.

  "newString": "The CLI keeps the polling secret in memory, displays the Broker-generated GitHub authorization URL, stores the returned credential in the system keyring, and acknowledges the credential only after storage succeeds.\n\n### Deploy the Auth Broker\n\nThe Broker is the FastAPI application in `auth_broker/`. Configure the GitHub App client and callback URL:\n\n| Variable | Required | Description |\n|:---|:---|:---|\n| `GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_ID` | Yes | GitHub App client ID. |\n| `GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_SECRET` | Yes | GitHub App client secret. |\n| `BROKER_CALLBACK_URL` | Yes | Fixed HTTPS callback URL, for example `https://broker.example.com/auth/callback`. |\n| `BROKER_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` | No | GitHub token-exchange timeout in seconds. Default: `30`. |\n\nRun the Broker with:\n\n```bash\nuvicorn auth_broker.app:create_app --factory --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000\n```\n\nRate limits are enabled by default and stored in memory per process:\n\n| Variable | Default |\n|:---|:---|\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` | `true` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS` | `60` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_START_PER_IP` | `60` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_CALLBACK_PER_IP` | `120` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_POLL_PER_IP` | `240` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_POLL_PER_TRANSACTION` | `60` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_ACK_PER_IP` | `60` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_ACK_PER_TRANSACTION` | `10` |\n| `BROKER_RATE_LIMIT_FAILURES_PER_IP` | `30` |\n\nBlocked requests receive `429 Too Many Requests` with a `Retry-After` header; the browser callback returns an HTML `429` page.\n\nSet `BROKER_TRUSTED_PROXIES` to a comma-separated list of reverse-proxy IPs or CIDRs that are allowed to supply `X-Forwarded-For`. Leave it empty when clients connect directly. When configured, the Broker uses the rightmost untrusted forwarded address for per-IP limits.\n\nRate-limit counters are local to each process and are not shared between workers. Adjust the limits for shared NAT environments, or deploy a shared limiter if strict global limits are required.\n\n### 📋 List Saved Accounts"
}

<path>C:\AI-Projects\DREAMEROL-SYNC\docs\GITHUB_APP_AUTH_SPEC.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
169: ## 12. Broker implementation constraints — v1
170: 
171: - One HTTPS Broker instance.
172: - In-memory transaction storage.
173: - No database.
174: - No Redis.
175: - No multi-instance coordination.
176: - No permanent credential storage.
177: 
178: These are deliberate v1 constraints. In-memory transaction storage does not support horizontal scaling across independent Broker instances. Multi-instance deployment requires a separately reviewed shared-state design.
179: 
180: ## 13. Rate limiting and abuse protection
181: 
182: Rate limits are configurable deployment settings, not universal constants.
183: 
184: Apply controls at multiple levels:
185: 
186: - Per IP.
187: - Per transaction.
188: - Per endpoint.
189: - Repeated authentication failures and suspicious activity.
190: 
191: Account for shared NAT addresses. Multiple legitimate users may share one public IP, so IP-based limits must not be the only security control. Document deployment defaults and make them configurable. Avoid treating a shared IP as proof that requests belong to one user.
192: 
193: ## 14. CLI requirements
194: 
195: CLI must:
196: 
197: - Generate and retain the polling secret in memory.
198: - Start authentication and display or open the Broker-generated browser authorization URL.
199: - Poll using the transaction ID in the request body and polling secret in the dedicated HTTPS header.
200: - Handle pending, processing, token-ready, delivery-authorized, expired, failed, and cancelled outcomes.
201: - Write the credential to the OS Keyring.
202: - Send ACK only after successful Keyring storage.
203: - Never print or log the credential or polling secret.
204: - Report Keyring and authentication failures clearly.
205: 
206: If Keyring storage fails, do not send ACK. Allow a safe retry while the transaction remains valid.
207: 
208: ## 15. Out of scope for v1
209: 
210: - Database or Redis integration.
211: - Multiple Broker instances.
212: - Refresh-token workflow.
213: - Mandatory GitHub App installation for every repository.
214: - Permanent Broker-side credential storage.
215: - Rewriting existing repository synchronization.
216: - Unnecessary authentication abstractions.
217: - Any fallback that bypasses PKCE S256.
218: 
219: ## 16. Acceptance criteria
220: 
221: Implementation is ready for review when all of the following are true:
222: 
223: 1. CLI generates the polling secret and sends only its hash to `/auth/start`.
224: 2. Transaction ID alone cannot retrieve a credential or authorize ACK.
225: 3. PKCE S256 is enforced with no fallback; Broker generates and retains the verifier per transaction and sends the matching S256 challenge.
226: 4. Callback uses a fixed HTTPS redirect URI with wildcard matching disabled.
227: 5. Missing or invalid `state` cannot modify an arbitrary transaction.
228: 6. Valid callback atomically transitions the transaction to `PROCESSING` before token exchange.
229: 7. OAuth denial and other callback errors terminate the associated transaction safely and do not leave it pending indefinitely.
230: 8. Broker validates token endpoint HTTP status, `Content-Type`, JSON syntax, schema, and token fields before making the credential available.
231: 9. The approved Opt-out expiration policy is enforced; unexpected expiring responses are rejected and no refresh fallback occurs.
232: 10. CLI can retrieve the credential using the polling secret.
233: 11. Credential is successfully stored in the OS Keyring before ACK.
234: 12. Broker rejects ACK before `DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED`; ACK is a client assertion, not independent Keyring verification.
235: 13. Successful ACK atomically removes the credential and prevents any poll begun after ACK from receiving it.
236: 14. Duplicate and concurrent poll, callback, and ACK requests are handled safely.
237: 15. Credentials and secrets never appear in logs or URLs.
238: 16. The approved GitHub permission matrix is preserved.
239: 17. Existing repository synchronization continues to work without changes to its safety behavior.
240: 18. Rate limits are configurable and account for shared NAT environments.

(End of file - total 240 lines)
</content>
```

1: """In-memory rate limiting and client-IP helpers for the authentication Broker."""
2: 
3: from __future__ import annotations
4: 
5: import ipaddress
6: import math
7: import threading
8: import time
9: from collections import deque
10: from dataclasses import dataclass
11: from functools import lru_cache
12: from typing import Callable, Iterable
13: 
14: from starlette.requests import Request
15: 
16: 
17: @dataclass(frozen=True)
18: class RateLimitPolicy:
19:     """A sliding-window request limit."""
20: 
21:     limit: int
22:     window_seconds: float
23: 
24:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
25:         if (
26:             isinstance(self.limit, bool)
27:             or not isinstance(self.limit, int)
28:             or self.limit <= 0
29:         ):
30:             raise ValueError("limit must be a positive integer")
31:         if (
32:             isinstance(self.window_seconds, bool)
33:             or not isinstance(self.window_seconds, (int, float))
34:             or not math.isfinite(self.window_seconds)
35:             or self.window_seconds <= 0
36:         ):
37:             raise ValueError("window_seconds must be a positive finite number")
38: 
39: 
40: class InMemoryRateLimiter:
41:     """Thread-safe sliding-window limiter for one Broker process."""
42: 
43:     def __init__(
44:         self,
45:         *,
46:         clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
47:         cleanup_threshold: int = 10_000,
48:     ) -> None:
49:         if (
50:             isinstance(cleanup_threshold, bool)
51:             or not isinstance(cleanup_threshold, int)
52:             or cleanup_threshold < 1
53:         ):
54:             raise ValueError("cleanup_threshold must be a positive integer")
55:         self._clock = clock
56:         self._events: dict[str, deque[float]] = {}
57:         self._windows: dict[str, float] = {}
58:         self._lock = threading.Lock()
59:         self._cleanup_threshold = cleanup_threshold
60:         self._operations = 0
61: 
62:     def retry_after(self, key: str, policy: RateLimitPolicy) -> float | None:
63:         """Return seconds until the key may proceed, without recording a request."""
64:         _require_key(key)
65:         now = self._clock()
66:         with self._lock:
67:             events = self._prune_locked(key, now, policy.window_seconds)
68:             if len(events) >= policy.limit:
69:                 return max(0.0, events[0] + policy.window_seconds - now)
70:             return None
71: 
72:     def record(self, key: str, policy: RateLimitPolicy) -> float | None:
73:         """Record a request or return seconds until the key may proceed."""
74:         _require_key(key)
75:         now = self._clock()
76:         with self._lock:
77:             events = self._prune_locked(key, now, policy.window_seconds)
78:             if len(events) >= policy.limit:
79:                 return max(0.0, events[0] + policy.window_seconds - now)
80:             events.append(now)
81:             self._operations += 1
82:             if (
83:                 self._operations % 1000 == 0
84:                 and len(self._events) > self._cleanup_threshold
85:             ):
86:                 self._cleanup_locked(now)
87:             return None
88: 
89:     def cleanup(self) -> int:
90:         """Remove expired keys and return how many were removed."""
91:         now = self._clock()
92:         with self._lock:
93:             return self._cleanup_locked(now)
94: 
95:     def clear(self) -> None:
96:         """Remove all limiter state, primarily for tests."""
97:         with self._lock:
98:             self._events.clear()
99:             self._windows.clear()
100:             self._operations = 0
101: 
102:     def _prune_locked(
103:         self,
104:         key: str,
105:         now: float,
106:         window_seconds: float,
107:     ) -> deque[float]:
108:         events = self._events.get(key)
109:         if events is None:
110:             events = deque()
111:             self._events[key] = events
112:         self._windows[key] = window_seconds
113:         cutoff = now - window_seconds
114:         while events and events[0] <= cutoff:
115:             events.popleft()
116:         return events
117: 
118:     def _cleanup_locked(self, now: float) -> int:
119:         removed = 0
120:         for key, events in list(self._events.items()):
121:             window_seconds = self._windows.get(key, 60.0)
122:             cutoff = now - window_seconds
123:             while events and events[0] <= cutoff:
124:                 events.popleft()
125:             if not events:
126:                 del self._events[key]
127:                 self._windows.pop(key, None)
128:                 removed += 1
129:         return removed
130: 
131: 
132: def get_client_ip(request: Request, trusted_proxies: Iterable[str] = ()) -> str:
133:     """Resolve the client IP, trusting forwarded headers only from configured proxies."""
134:     peer = request.client.host if request.client else "unknown"
135:     networks = _trusted_networks(tuple(trusted_proxies))
136:     if not _is_trusted(peer, networks):
137:         return peer
138: 
139:     forwarded = request.headers.get("x-forwarded-for")
140:     if not forwarded:
141:         return peer
142: 
143:     candidates = [
144:         parsed
145:         for part in forwarded.split(",")
146:         if (parsed := _parse_ip(part.strip())) is not None
147:     ]
148:     for candidate in reversed(candidates):
149:         if not _is_trusted(str(candidate), networks):
150:             return str(candidate)
151:     if candidates:
152:         return str(candidates[0])
153:     return peer
154: 
155: 
156: @lru_cache(maxsize=32)
157: def _trusted_networks(
158:     values: tuple[str, ...],
159: ) -> tuple[ipaddress.IPv4Network | ipaddress.IPv6Network, ...]:
160:     return tuple(ipaddress.ip_network(value, strict=False) for value in values)
161: 
162: 
163: def _parse_ip(value: str) -> ipaddress.IPv4Address | ipaddress.IPv6Address | None:
164:     try:
165:         parsed = ipaddress.ip_address(value)
166:     except ValueError:
167:         return None
168:     if isinstance(parsed, ipaddress.IPv6Address) and parsed.ipv4_mapped:
169:         return parsed.ipv4_mapped
170:     return parsed
171: 
172: 
173: def _is_trusted(
174:     value: str,
175:     networks: tuple[ipaddress.IPv4Network | ipaddress.IPv6Network, ...],
176: ) -> bool:
177:     parsed = _parse_ip(value)
178:     if parsed is None:
179:         return False
180:     return any(parsed in network for network in networks)
181: 
182: 
183: def _require_key(key: str) -> None:
184:     if not isinstance(key, str) or not key:
185:         raise ValueError("key must be a non-empty string")
186: 
187: 
188: __all__ = [
189:     "InMemoryRateLimiter",
190:     "RateLimitPolicy",
191:     "get_client_ip",
192: ]

_Thinking:_

Potential gap: Keyring storage fails -> CLI returns 1, no retry. Spec says "Allow a safe retry while the transaction remains valid." Does that mean CLI should allow retry automatically? It says "If Keyring storage fails, do not send ACK. Allow a safe retry while the transaction remains valid." Current behavior leaves transaction valid but CLI exits. User could rerun `--login` but that starts a new transaction. The old credential remains until TTL. Is that "allow a safe retry"? Not really. Could be a gap. We can propose next step: fix Keyring retry within same transaction. That's concrete and spec-based.

Also `AUTH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 600` while transaction TTL? Need check default TTL maybe 600. Fine.

Potential gap: `_run_broker_login` `finally` closes client. Fine.
Potential gap: It doesn't handle `KeyboardInterrupt` / user cancellation; not required.
Potential gap: `client.poll` only catches `BrokerAuthError`; if `requests` returns 429, `BrokerAuthError` message "HTTP 429", CLI exits. Maybe okay. Could retry on 429? Not required. But rate limiting now means CLI might hit 429? Poll interval 2s, default 240/min, no. Fine.
Potential gap: `client.ack` failure returns 0 even though ACK failed; spec says ACK after storage, but if ACK fails, credential stored. Returning 0 maybe okay, but user might think fully done. Could be a gap: "Broker could not be notified" but exit 0. Should exit nonzero? Not specified. Could be considered. But next step audit.

Potential gap: `AuthPoll` doesn't validate `state` against known states; CLI handles unexpected. Fine.
Potential gap: `_json_object` doesn't check Content-Type; spec token endpoint validation only. Broker responses? Not required.
Potential gap: `client.start` doesn't validate transaction ID format; maybe okay.
Potential gap: `client.poll` sends polling secret header over HTTPS; okay.
Potential gap: `client.ack` uses same header. Good.
Potential gap: `client.poll` if state `DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED` but `access_token` empty -> error. Good.
Potential gap: CLI doesn't explicitly zero polling secret; Python memory. Not required.
Potential gap: CLI prints authorization URL (contains client_id, state, PKCE challenge, redirect_uri, scope). No secret. Good.
Potential gap: Broker `authorization_url` may include state (transaction ID) and code challenge. Fine.

Potential gap: Callback errors. Need inspect routes callback to see if OAuth denial terminates.

1: """Thread-safe, in-memory authentication transaction state machine.
2: 
3: This module deliberately has no persistence or OAuth/network dependencies. A

25: 
26: class TransactionState(str, Enum):
27:     """States permitted by the approved authentication flow."""
28: 
29:     PENDING = "PENDING"
30:     PROCESSING = "PROCESSING"
31:     TOKEN_READY = "TOKEN_READY"
32:     DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED = "DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED"
33:     ACKNOWLEDGED = "ACKNOWLEDGED"

35:     CANCELLED = "CANCELLED"
36:     EXPIRED = "EXPIRED"

39: class TransactionError(Exception):
40:     """Base exception for transaction operations."""
41: 
42: 
43: class InvalidTransaction(TransactionError):
44:     """The transaction is unknown or the supplied polling secret is invalid."""
45: 
46: 
47: class InvalidCallbackState(TransactionError):
48:     """The callback state does not identify a valid transaction."""
49: 
50: 
51: class InvalidTransition(TransactionError):
52:     """The requested operation is not valid for the transaction's state."""
53: 
54: 
55: class TransactionExpired(TransactionError):
56:     """The transaction has expired."""

59: @dataclass(frozen=True)

62: 
63:     state: TransactionState

67: @dataclass
68: class _Transaction:
69:     transaction_id: str
70:     polling_secret_hash: str
71:     callback_state_hash: str
72:     created_at: float
73:     expires_at: float
74:     state: TransactionState = TransactionState.PENDING
75:     credential: str | None = None
76:     code_verifier: str | None = field(default=None, repr=False)
77: 
78: 
79: class TransactionStore:
80:     """Atomic in-memory transaction store for one Broker process."""
81: 
82:     def __init__(
83:         self,
84:         ttl_seconds: float = 600,
85:         *,
86:         clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
87:     ) -> None:
88:         if isinstance(ttl_seconds, bool) or not isinstance(ttl_seconds, (int, float)):
89:             raise ValueError("ttl_seconds must be a positive finite number")
90:         try:
91:             ttl_seconds = float(ttl_seconds)
92:         except (OverflowError, TypeError, ValueError):
93:             raise ValueError("ttl_seconds must be a positive finite number") from None
94:         if not math.isfinite(ttl_seconds) or ttl_seconds <= 0:
95:             raise ValueError("ttl_seconds must be a positive finite number")
96:         self._ttl_seconds = ttl_seconds
97:         self._clock = clock
98:         self._lock = threading.RLock()
99:         self._transactions: dict[str, _Transaction] = {}
100:         self._callback_states: dict[str, str] = {}
101:         # Hash -> original transaction expiry. Never retain callback plaintext.
102:         self._used_callback_states: dict[str, float] = {}
103: 
104:     def create(
105:         self,
106:         polling_secret_hash: str,
107:         callback_state: str,
108:         *,
109:         code_verifier: str | None = None,
110:     ) -> str:
111:         """Create a PENDING transaction, storing only hashes of supplied secrets."""
112:         if not isinstance(polling_secret_hash, str) or not _HASH_PATTERN.fullmatch(
113:             polling_secret_hash
114:         ):
115:             raise ValueError("polling_secret_hash must be a lowercase SHA-256 hex digest")
116:         if not isinstance(callback_state, str) or not callback_state:
117:             raise ValueError("callback_state must be a non-empty string")
118:         if code_verifier is not None and (
119:             not isinstance(code_verifier, str) or not code_verifier
120:         ):
121:             raise ValueError("code_verifier must be a non-empty string")
122: 
123:         callback_state_hash = hash_secret(callback_state)
124:         with self._lock:
125:             now = self._clock()
126:             self._cleanup_expired_locked(now)
127:             if (
128:                 callback_state_hash in self._callback_states
129:                 or callback_state_hash in self._used_callback_states
130:             ):
131:                 raise ValueError("callback state has already been used")
132:             transaction_id = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
133:             while transaction_id in self._transactions:
134:                 transaction_id = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
135:             transaction = _Transaction(
136:                 transaction_id=transaction_id,
137:                 polling_secret_hash=polling_secret_hash,
138:                 callback_state_hash=callback_state_hash,
139:                 created_at=now,
140:                 expires_at=now + self._ttl_seconds,
141:                 code_verifier=code_verifier,
142:             )
143:             self._transactions[transaction_id] = transaction
144:             self._callback_states[callback_state_hash] = transaction_id
145:             return transaction_id
146: 
147:     def claim_callback(self, callback_state: str) -> str:
148:         """Atomically claim a valid callback once, transitioning PENDING to PROCESSING."""
149:         if not isinstance(callback_state, str) or not callback_state:
150:             raise InvalidCallbackState("invalid callback state")
151:         supplied_hash = hash_secret(callback_state)
152:         with self._lock:
153:             transaction_id = self._callback_states.get(supplied_hash)
154:             if transaction_id is None:
155:                 raise InvalidCallbackState("invalid callback state")
156:             transaction = self._transactions.get(transaction_id)
157:             if transaction is None or not hmac.compare_digest(
158:                 supplied_hash, transaction.callback_state_hash
159:             ):
160:                 raise InvalidCallbackState("invalid callback state")
161:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
162:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
163:                 self._callback_states.pop(supplied_hash, None)
164:                 raise TransactionExpired("transaction has expired")
165:             if transaction.state is not TransactionState.PENDING:
166:                 raise InvalidTransition("callback has already been claimed or completed")
167:             transaction.state = TransactionState.PROCESSING
168:             # Retain this digest until the original transaction expiry so the
169:             # same callback state cannot be re-used for a new transaction.
170:             self._used_callback_states[supplied_hash] = transaction.expires_at
171:             self._callback_states.pop(supplied_hash, None)
172:             return transaction_id
173: 
174:     def fail_callback(self, callback_state: str, *, cancelled: bool = False) -> str:
175:         """Fail/cancel a callback only after authenticating its transaction state."""
176:         if not isinstance(callback_state, str) or not callback_state:
177:             raise InvalidCallbackState("invalid callback state")
178:         supplied_hash = hash_secret(callback_state)
179:         with self._lock:
180:             transaction_id = self._callback_states.get(supplied_hash)
181:             if transaction_id is None:
182:                 raise InvalidCallbackState("invalid callback state")
183:             transaction = self._transactions.get(transaction_id)
184:             if transaction is None or not hmac.compare_digest(
185:                 supplied_hash, transaction.callback_state_hash
186:             ):
187:                 raise InvalidCallbackState("invalid callback state")
188:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
189:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
190:                 self._callback_states.pop(supplied_hash, None)
191:                 raise TransactionExpired("transaction has expired")
192:             if transaction.state is not TransactionState.PENDING:
193:                 raise InvalidTransition("callback is not pending")
194:             transaction.code_verifier = None
195:             transaction.state = (
196:                 TransactionState.CANCELLED if cancelled else TransactionState.FAILED
197:             )
198:             self._used_callback_states[supplied_hash] = transaction.expires_at
199:             self._callback_states.pop(supplied_hash, None)
200:             return transaction_id
201: 
202:     def fail_processing(self, transaction_id: str) -> TransactionState:
203:         """Atomically fail a claimed callback after exchange/validation failure."""
204:         with self._lock:
205:             transaction = self._get_transaction(transaction_id)
206:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
207:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
208:                 raise TransactionExpired("transaction has expired")
209:             if transaction.state is not TransactionState.PROCESSING:
210:                 raise InvalidTransition("only PROCESSING transactions can fail")
211:             transaction.credential = None
212:             transaction.code_verifier = None
213:             transaction.state = TransactionState.FAILED
214:             self._callback_states.pop(transaction.callback_state_hash, None)
215:             return transaction.state
216: 
217:     def get_code_verifier(self, transaction_id: str) -> str:
218:         """Return the PKCE verifier only while the transaction is PROCESSING."""
219:         with self._lock:
220:             transaction = self._get_transaction(transaction_id)
221:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
222:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
223:                 raise TransactionExpired("transaction has expired")
224:             if transaction.state is not TransactionState.PROCESSING:
225:                 raise InvalidTransition(
226:                     "code verifier is available only during PROCESSING"
227:                 )
228:             if transaction.code_verifier is None:
229:                 raise InvalidTransition("transaction has no code verifier")
230:             return transaction.code_verifier
231: 
232:     def make_token_ready(self, transaction_id: str, credential: str) -> None:
233:         """Store a validated credential after callback processing succeeds."""
234:         if not isinstance(credential, str) or not credential:
235:             raise ValueError("credential must be a non-empty string")
236:         with self._lock:
237:             transaction = self._get_transaction(transaction_id)
238:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
239:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
240:                 raise TransactionExpired("transaction has expired")
241:             if transaction.state is not TransactionState.PROCESSING:
242:                 raise InvalidTransition("only PROCESSING transactions can become TOKEN_READY")
243:             transaction.credential = credential
244:             transaction.code_verifier = None
245:             transaction.state = TransactionState.TOKEN_READY
246: 
247:     def poll(self, transaction_id: str, polling_secret: str) -> PollResult:
248:         """Authenticate a poll and atomically authorize/repeat token delivery."""
249:         with self._lock:
250:             transaction = self._get_authenticated(transaction_id, polling_secret)
251:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
252:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
253:                 return PollResult(TransactionState.EXPIRED)
254:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.TOKEN_READY:
255:                 if transaction.credential is None:
256:                     raise InvalidTransition("TOKEN_READY transaction has no credential")
257:                 transaction.state = TransactionState.DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED
258:                 return PollResult(transaction.state, transaction.credential)
259:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED:
260:                 if transaction.credential is None:
261:                     raise InvalidTransition("DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED transaction has no credential")
262:                 return PollResult(transaction.state, transaction.credential)
263:             return PollResult(transaction.state)
264: 
265:     def acknowledge(self, transaction_id: str, polling_secret: str) -> TransactionState:
266:         """Acknowledge delivery; only DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED transactions can ACK."""
267:         with self._lock:
268:             transaction = self._get_authenticated(transaction_id, polling_secret)
269:             self._expire_if_needed(transaction)
270:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.EXPIRED:
271:                 raise TransactionExpired("transaction has expired")
272:             if transaction.state is TransactionState.ACKNOWLEDGED:
273:                 transaction.code_verifier = None
274:                 return TransactionState.ACKNOWLEDGED
275:             if transaction.state is not TransactionState.DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED:
276:                 raise InvalidTransition("ACK is allowed only after delivery is authorized")
277:             transaction.credential = None
278:             transaction.code_verifier = None
279:             transaction.state = TransactionState.ACKNOWLEDGED
280:             return transaction.state
281: 
282:     def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
283:         """Remove expired transactions and callback indexes; return removed count."""
284:         with self._lock:
285:             return self._cleanup_expired_locked(self._clock())
286: 
287:     def _cleanup_expired_locked(self, now: float) -> int:
288:         """Purge expired transactions and callback tombstones; caller holds lock."""
289:         expired_ids = [
290:             transaction_id
291:             for transaction_id, transaction in self._transactions.items()
292:             if now >= transaction.expires_at
293:         ]
294:         for transaction_id in expired_ids:
295:             transaction = self._transactions.pop(transaction_id)
296:             transaction.credential = None
297:             transaction.code_verifier = None
298:             self._callback_states.pop(transaction.callback_state_hash, None)
299: 
300:         expired_state_hashes = [
301:             state_hash
302:             for state_hash, expires_at in self._used_callback_states.items()
303:             if now >= expires_at
304:         ]
305:         for state_hash in expired_state_hashes:
306:             self._used_callback_states.pop(state_hash, None)
307: 
308:         return len(expired_ids)
309: 
310:     def _get_authenticated(self, transaction_id: str, polling_secret: str) -> _Transaction:
311:         transaction = self._transactions.get(transaction_id)
312:         if transaction is None:
313:             raise InvalidTransaction("invalid transaction or polling secret")
314:         if not isinstance(polling_secret, str) or not polling_secret:
315:             raise InvalidTransaction("invalid transaction or polling secret")
316:         supplied_hash = hash_secret(polling_secret)
317:         if not hmac.compare_digest(supplied_hash, transaction.polling_secret_hash):
318:             raise InvalidTransaction("invalid transaction or polling secret")
319:         return transaction
320: 
321:     def _get_transaction(self, transaction_id: str) -> _Transaction:
322:         transaction = self._transactions.get(transaction_id)
323:         if transaction is None:
324:             raise InvalidTransaction("unknown transaction")
325:         return transaction
326: 
327:     def _expire_if_needed(self, transaction: _Transaction) -> None:
328:         if self._clock() >= transaction.expires_at:
329:             transaction.credential = None
330:             transaction.code_verifier = None
331:             transaction.state = TransactionState.EXPIRED
332:             self._callback_states.pop(transaction.callback_state_hash, None)

(End of file - total 332 lines)
</content>

138: ## 10. GitHub repository access and permissions
139: 
140: Do not assume every repository requires a GitHub App installation.
141: 
142: Use the approved user-to-server access-token model. Do not conflate user-to-server tokens with installation tokens or introduce mandatory installation steps unless the product explicitly adopts that model.
143: 
144: A user access token can access repository resources only within accounts where the GitHub App is installed, and only where both the App and the user have access. Installation is account-scoped; do not require a separate installation for every repository.
145: 
146: The approved GitHub permission matrix for the five identified API operations remains normative:
147: 
148: | Operation | REST endpoint | Minimum GitHub App repository permission |
149: |---|---|---|
150: | Identify authenticated user | `GET /user` | No additional permissions |
151: | Discover accessible repositories | `GET /user/repos` | `Metadata: read` |
152: | Retrieve repository metadata and fork parent information | `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}` | `Metadata: read` |
153: | Compare upstream and fork branches | `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare/{basehead}` | `Contents: read` |
154: | Synchronize fork with upstream | `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/merge-upstream` | `Contents: write` |
155: 
156: The configured App permission is `Metadata: Read-only` and `Contents: Read and write`. This does not mean every operation requires write access. App permissions and the user's actual access to a repository are distinct. Preserve these permission and scope decisions. Do not broaden them or leave the matrix open to interpretation during implementation.
157: 
158: Any proposed permission change must be justified against the actual API operation and reviewed before implementation. Do not introduce installation access tokens or mandatory installation steps without an approved product decision.
159: 
160: ## 11. Existing synchronization code
161: 
162: The existing code has been inspected:
163: 
164: - `fork_sync/github.py` contains the GitHub API client and uses the five identified GitHub endpoints.
165: - `main.py` delegates to `GitHubClient` and `ForkSynchronizer`.
166: 
167: Authentication changes must preserve existing synchronization behavior. Do not rewrite working synchronization logic as part of this task. Keep authentication responsibilities separate from repository synchronization. Any change to existing GitHub API calls must be justified by a concrete requirement and checked against the approved permission matrix.

172: - In-memory transaction storage.

                            response = "cancel"
                        if response.strip():

def test_transaction_and_poll_result_repr_hide_credential_and_hashes(store_and_secrets):
    store, _, transaction_id, polling_secret, callback_state = store_and_secrets
    store.claim_callback(callback_state)

        assert "credential-value" not in rendered
    assert transaction.polling_secret_hash not in repr(transaction)

    assert polling_secret not in repr(transaction)

3. PKCE S256 enforced no fallback; Broker generates verifier, sends challenge. `oauth.py` uses S256, stores verifier; tests. Good.
4. Callback fixed HTTPS redirect URI with wildcard disabled. `BrokerConfig` validates; tests. Good.
5. Missing/invalid state cannot modify arbitrary transaction. `TransactionStore` hash lookup; tests. Good.
6. Valid callback atomically transitions to PROCESSING before token exchange. `claim_callback` then exchange. Good.
7. OAuth denial/callback errors terminate safely. `fail_callback`/`fail_processing`; tests? likely. Good.
8. Broker validates token endpoint status, Content-Type, JSON syntax, schema, fields. `token_policy`; tests. Good.
9. Expiration policy enforced; unexpected expiring rejected. `token_policy`; tests. Good.
10. CLI retrieve credential using polling secret. `client.poll` header; tests/E2E. Good.
11. Credential stored in OS Keyring before ACK. `main.py`; tests. Good.
12. Broker rejects ACK before DELIVERY_AUTHORIZED. `acknowledge`; tests. Good.
13. Successful ACK removes credential and prevents poll after ACK. `acknowledge`; tests. Good.
14. Duplicate/concurrent poll/callback/ACK safe. Tests. Good.
15. Credentials/secrets never in logs or URLs. No logging; CLI tests. But repr gap fixed. Any other URLs? `authorization_url` includes state (not secret) and code_challenge (not verifier). Callback state is transaction identifier, not secret? It is a CSRF token; spec says state. It appears in URL by OAuth design. "Credentials and secrets never appear in URLs" - state is a secret? The callback state is a secret-ish CSRF value; it appears in URL to GitHub and callback. But OAuth state necessarily in URL. Spec likely means polling secret/credential. Good.
16. Permission matrix preserved. No changes to GitHub API; auth broker uses OAuth only. Good.
17. Existing sync behavior unchanged. Step 5; tests. Good.
18. Rate limits configurable/shared NAT. Step 6; tests/docs. Good.

Let's consider the phrase: "If Keyring storage fails, do not send ACK. Allow a safe retry while the transaction remains valid." The key requirements: (a) no ACK on failure, (b) safe retry while valid. Our implementation allows retry; if transaction expires, ACK fails but storage may succeed. Is that a violation? It says "while the transaction remains valid" could mean the retry mechanism should only be available while valid. We don't check. We could call `client.poll` before retry to check. But if expired, poll returns EXPIRED (200) and no credential. We could then not store. That would be safest.
